**Tool Result:** `toolu_vrtx_01N6gToTHq6pAJ8Ug8Zt3yhF`

```
Tree-sitter language grammars are configured and loaded through a multi-layered system in `src/syntax/`.

### 1. Language Configuration
Languages are defined in an enum with mapping from file extensions and filenames to language IDs.
- [src/syntax/languages.rs#L7-L123](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/languages.rs#L7-L123) - `LanguageId` enum and `from_extension`/`from_path` detection logic.

### 2. Grammar Loading and Initialization
The `ParserState` struct manages the lifecycle of tree-sitter parsers and queries.
- [src/syntax/parser.rs#L93-L126](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/parser.rs#L93-L126) - Grammar queries are either embedded via `include_str!` from the `queries/` directory or loaded from built-in constants in tree-sitter crates (e.g., `tree-sitter-rust::HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY`).
- [src/syntax/parser.rs#L141-L212](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/parser.rs#L141-L212) - `ParserState::new()` initializes supported languages in phases.
- [src/syntax/parser.rs#L215-L273](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/parser.rs#L215-L273) - `init_language` maps `LanguageId` to specific tree-sitter grammar objects and compiles the associated SCM queries.

### 3. Syntax Highlighting Queries
Queries are defined using Scheme-like `.scm` files and mapped to a set of standardized capture names.
- [queries/](file://~/code/token-editor/queries/) - Directory containing language-specific `highlights.scm` files (e.g., [javascript/highlights.scm](file://~/code/token-editor/queries/javascript/highlights.scm), [markdown/highlights.scm](file://~/code/token-editor/queries/markdown/highlights.scm)).
- [src/syntax/highlights.rs#L9-L48](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/highlights.rs#L9-L48) - `HIGHLIGHT_NAMES` constant defines the standard capture names (e.g., `@function`, `@keyword`, `@string`) used across all grammars.
- [src/syntax/highlights.rs#L131-L148](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/highlights.rs#L131-L148) - `highlight_id_for_name` provides hierarchical lookup for these captures.

### 4. Specialized Parsing
Some languages use complex loading strategies:
- [src/syntax/parser.rs#L190-L212](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/parser.rs#L190-L212) - **Markdown** uses a two-pass system with both `BLOCK` and `INLINE` grammars.
- [src/syntax/parser.rs#L295-L297](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/parser.rs#L295-L297) - **HTML** supports language injection for CSS and JavaScript (implemented in `parse_and_highlight_html`).
```

  "content": "1: //! Tree-sitter parser state and highlighting extraction\n2: //!\n3: //! Manages parsers, trees, and queries for syntax highlighting.\n4: //! Supports incremental parsing by caching trees and computing edits.\n5: \n6: use std::collections::HashMap;\n7: \n8: use streaming_iterator::StreamingIterator;\n9: use tree_sitter::{InputEdit, Parser, Point, Query, QueryCursor, Tree, TreeCursor};\n10: \n11: use super::highlights::{highlight_id_for_name, HighlightToken, SyntaxHighlights};\n12: use super::languages::LanguageId;\n13: use crate::model::editor_area::DocumentId;\n14: \n15: /// Cached parse state for a document (enables incremental parsing)\n16: struct DocParseState {\n17:     /// The language this tree was parsed with\n18:     language: LanguageId,\n19:     /// The parsed tree\n20:     tree: Tree,\n21:     /// The source text that was parsed (needed for computing edits)\n22:     source: String,\n23: }\n24: \n25: /// Convert byte column to character column (handles UTF-8 multi-byte chars)\n26: fn byte_to_char_col(text: &str, byte_col: usize) -> usize {\n27:     let byte_col = byte_col.min(text.len());\n28:     let mut valid_byte = byte_col;\n29:     while valid_byte > 0 && !text.is_char_boundary(valid_byte) {\n30:         valid_byte -= 1;\n31:     }\n32:     text[..valid_byte].chars().count()\n33: }\n34: \n35: /// Convert a byte offset to a tree-sitter Point (row, column in bytes)\n36: fn byte_to_point(text: &str, byte_offset: usize) -> Point {\n37:     let mut row = 0usize;\n38:     let mut col = 0usize;\n39:     let bytes = text.as_bytes();\n40: \n41:     for &byte in bytes.iter().take(byte_offset) {\n42:         if byte == b'\\n' {\n43:             row += 1;\n44:             col = 0;\n45:         } else {\n46:             col += 1;\n47:         }\n48:     }\n49: \n50:     Point { row, column: col }\n51: }\n52: \n53: /// Compute an InputEdit by diffing old and new source text.\n54: /// Returns None if the sources are identical.\n55: fn compute_incremental_edit(old_src: &str, new_src: &str) -> Option<InputEdit> {\n56:     if old_src == new_src {\n57:         return None;\n58:     }\n59: \n60:     let old_bytes = old_src.as_bytes();\n61:     let new_bytes = new_src.as_bytes();\n62: \n63:     // Find common prefix length (in bytes)\n64:     let mut start = 0;\n65:     let max_start = old_bytes.len().min(new_bytes.len());\n66:     while start < max_start && old_bytes[start] == new_bytes[start] {\n67:         start += 1;\n68:     }\n69: \n70:     // Find common suffix length (in bytes), not overlapping prefix\n71:     let mut old_end = old_bytes.len();\n72:     let mut new_end = new_bytes.len();\n73:     while old_end > start && new_end > start && old_bytes[old_end - 1] == new_bytes[new_end - 1] {\n74:         old_end -= 1;\n75:         new_end -= 1;\n76:     }\n77: \n78:     // The edit is: old_src[start..old_end] replaced by new_src[start..new_end]\n79:     let start_position = byte_to_point(old_src, start);\n80:     let old_end_position = byte_to_point(old_src, old_end);\n81:     let new_end_position = byte_to_point(new_src, new_end);\n82: \n83:     Some(InputEdit {\n84:         start_byte: start,\n85:         old_end_byte: old_end,\n86:         new_end_byte: new_end,\n87:         start_position,\n88:         old_end_position,\n89:         new_end_position,\n90:     })\n91: }\n92: \n93: // Embedded query files\n94: // Phase 1 languages\n95: const YAML_HIGHLIGHTS: &str =   "content": "1: ;; scheme-basics.sema — Demonstrates 5 standard Scheme features\n2: ;; Car/cdr compositions, association lists, do loops, characters, promises\n3: \n4: ;; ============================================================\n5: ;; 1. Car/Cdr Compositions\n6: ;; ============================================================\n7: \n8: (define nested '((1 2 3) (4 5 6) (7 8 9)))\n9: \n10: (println \"=== Car/Cdr Compositions ===\")\n11: (println (format \"nested:  ~s\" nested))\n12: (println (format \"caar:    ~a  (first of first)\" (caar nested)))\n13: (println (format \"cadr:    ~s  (second element)\" (cadr nested)))\n14: (println (format \"caddr:   ~s  (third element)\" (caddr nested)))\n15: (println (format \"cadar:   ~a  (second of first)\" (cadar nested)))\n16: (println (format \"cddr:    ~s  (drop two)\" (cddr nested)))\n17: \n18: ;; ============================================================\n19: ;; 2. Association Lists\n20: ;; ============================================================\n21: \n22: (define phonebook\n23:   '((\"Alice\"   \"555-1234\")\n24:     (\"Bob\"     \"555-5678\")\n25:     (\"Charlie\" \"555-9012\")))\n26: \n27: (println \"\\n=== Association Lists ===\")\n28: (println (format \"Phonebook: ~s\" phonebook))\n29: \n30: (define entry (assoc \"Bob\" phonebook))\n31: (println (format \"assoc \\\"Bob\\\":     ~s\" entry))\n32: (println (format \"Bob's number:   ~a\" (cadr entry)))\n33: \n34: (define missing (assoc \"Dave\" phonebook))\n35: (println (format \"assoc \\\"Dave\\\":    ~a\" missing))\n36: \n37: ;; Keyword alist with assq\n38: (define config '((:host \"localhost\") (:port 8080) (:debug #t)))\n39: (println (format \"assq :port:     ~s\" (assq :port config)))\n40: \n41: ;; ============================================================\n42: ;; 3. Do Loops\n43: ;; ============================================================\n44: \n45: (println \"\\n=== Do Loops ===\")\n46: \n47: ;; Sum 1..10\n48: (define sum\n49:   (do ((i 1 (+ i 1))\n50:        (acc 0 (+ acc i)))\n51:     ((> i 10) acc)))\n52: (println (format \"Sum 1..10:   ~a\" sum))\n53: \n54: ;; Factorial\n55: (define fact\n56:   (do ((n 10 (- n 1))\n57:        (acc 1 (* acc n)))\n58:     ((= n 0) acc)))\n59: (println (format \"10!:         ~a\" fact))\n60: \n61: ;; FizzBuzz 1..15\n62: (println \"FizzBuzz 1..15:\")\n63: (do ((i 1 (+ i 1)))\n64:   ((> i 15))\n65:   (println (format \"  ~a: ~a\" i\n66:     (cond\n67:       ((= 0 (math/remainder i 15)) \"FizzBuzz\")\n68:       ((= 0 (math/remainder i 3))  \"Fizz\")\n69:       ((= 0 (math/remainder i 5))  \"Buzz\")\n70:       (else                         i)))))\n71: \n72: ;; ============================================================\n73: ;; 4. Characters\n74: ;; ============================================================\n75: \n76: (println \"\\n=== Characters ===\")\n77: \n78: ;; Literals\n79: (println (format \"Char literal:  ~s\" #\\a))\n80: (println (format \"Space:         ~s\" #\\space))\n81: (println (format \"Newline:       ~s\" #\\newline))\n82: \n83: ;; Predicates\n84: (println (format \"alphabetic? #\\\\A: ~a\" (char-alphabetic? #\\A)))\n85: (println (format \"numeric? #\\\\5:    ~a\" (char-numeric? #\\5)))\n86: (println (format \"whitespace? ' ': ~a\" (char-whitespace? #\\space)))\n87: (println (format \"upper-case? #\\\\A: ~a\" (char-upper-case? #\\A)))\n88: (println (format \"lower-case? #\\\\a: ~a\" (char-lower-case? #\\a)))\n89: \n90: ;; Case conversion\n91: (println (format \"upcase #\\\\a:   ~s\" (char-upcase #\\a)))\n92: (println (format \"downcase #\\\\Z: ~s\" (char-downcase #\\Z)))\n93: \n94: ;; String <-> Char conversions\n95: (define chars (string->list \"Hello\"))\n96: (println (format \"string->list \\\"Hello\\\": ~s\" chars))\n97: (println (format \"list->string back:    ~a\" (list->string chars)))\n98: \n99: ;; char->integer / integer->char\n100: (println (format \"char->integer #\\\\A: ~a\" (char->integer #\\A)))\n101: (println (format \"integer->char 65:  ~s\" (integer->char 65)))\n102: \n103: ;; ============================================================\n104: ;; 5. Promises (Lazy Evaluation)\n105: ;; ============================================================\n106: \n107: (println \"\\n=== Promises (delay/force) ===\")\n108: \n109: ;; Basic\n110: (define p (delay (begin (println \"  Computing...\") (* 6 7))))\n111: (println (format \"promise? p:        ~a\" (promise? p)))\n112: (println (format \"promise-forced? p: ~a\" (promise-forced? p)))\n113: (println \"Forcing p:\")\n114: (define result (force p))\n115: (println (format \"Result:            ~a\" result))\n116: (println (format \"promise-forced? p: ~a\" (promise-forced? p)))\n117: \n118: ;; Memoization: body runs only once\n119: (define counter 0)\n120: (define memo-p (delay (begin (set! counter (+ counter 1)) counter)))\n121: (println (format \"\\nFirst force:  ~a\" (force memo-p)))\n122: (println (format \"Second force: ~a\" (force memo-p)))\n123: (println (format \"Counter:      ~a (only incremented once)\" counter))\n124: \n125: ;; Force on non-promise passes through (R7RS)\n126: (println (format \"\\n(force 42):   ~a\" (force 42)))\n127: \n128: ;; Lazy fibonacci sequence element\n129: (define (lazy-add a b) (delay (+ (force a) (force b))))\n130: (define fib-a (delay 0))\n131: (define fib-b (delay 1))\n132: (define fib-c (lazy-add fib-a fib-b))\n133: (define fib-d (lazy-add fib-b fib-c))\n134: (define fib-e (lazy-add fib-c fib-d))\n135: (println (format \"Lazy fib(4):  ~a\" (force fib-e)))\n136: \n137: (println \"\\nDone!\")",
  "absolutePath": "~/code/sema-lisp/examples/scheme-basics.sema"
}

{
  "content": "1: ; Sema highlight queries for Helix\n2: ; Helix uses LAST-MATCH-WINS semantics, so generic fallbacks go FIRST\n3: ; and increasingly specific patterns follow.\n4: ; See: https://docs.helix-editor.com/themes.html for capture names.\n5: \n6: ; =====================================================================\n7: ; GENERIC FALLBACKS (least specific — put first so they lose to later)\n8: ; =====================================================================\n9: \n10: ; --- Fallback: any remaining symbol is a variable ---\n11: \n12: (symbol) @variable\n13: \n14: ; --- Generic function call ---\n15: \n16: (list\n17:   .\n18:   (symbol) @function)\n19: \n20: ; =====================================================================\n21: ; INCREASINGLY SPECIFIC PATTERNS (override the fallbacks above)\n22: ; =====================================================================\n23: \n24: ; --- Literals ---\n25: \n26: (number) @constant.numeric\n27: (boolean) @constant.builtin.boolean\n28: (character) @constant.character\n29: (string) @string\n30: (escape_sequence) @constant.character.escape\n31: \n32: ; --- Comments ---\n33: \n34: (comment) @comment.line\n35: (block_comment) @comment.block\n36: ; --- Punctuation ---\n37: \n38: [\"(\" \")\" \"[\" \"]\" \"{\" \"}\"] @punctuation.bracket\n39: \n40: ; --- Quote operators ---\n41: \n42: (quote \"'\") @operator\n43: (unquote_splicing \",@\") @operator\n44: (unquote \",\") @operator\n45: (quasiquote \"`\") @operator\n46: \n47: ; --- Special variables ---\n48: \n49: ((symbol) @variable.builtin\n50:   (#any-of? @variable.builtin \"...\" \".\"))\n51: \n52: ; --- Operators ---\n53: \n54: ((symbol) @operator\n55:   (#any-of? @operator \"+\" \"-\" \"*\" \"/\" \"=\" \">\" \"<\" \">=\" \"<=\" \"eq?\" \"equal?\"))\n56: \n57: ; --- Keyword literals :foo ---\n58: \n59: ((symbol) @string.special.symbol\n60:   (#match? @string.special.symbol \"^:\"))\n61: \n62: ; --- nil ---\n63: \n64: ((symbol) @constant.builtin\n65:   (#eq? @constant.builtin \"nil\"))\n66: \n67: ; --- Boolean symbols (Scheme grammar parses true/false as symbols) ---\n68: \n69: ((symbol) @constant.builtin.boolean\n70:   (#any-of? @constant.builtin.boolean \"true\" \"false\"))\n71: \n72: ; --- Define simple variable: (define name value) ---\n73: \n74: (list\n75:   .\n76:   (symbol) @_f\n77:   .\n78:   (symbol) @variable\n79:   (#eq? @_f \"define\"))\n80: \n81: ; --- Define function: (defun name ...) ---\n82: \n83: (list\n84:   .\n85:   (symbol) @_f\n86:   .\n87:   (symbol) @function\n88:   (#eq? @_f \"defun\"))\n89: \n90: ; --- Lambda / fn parameter lists ---\n91: \n92: (list\n93:   .\n94:   (symbol) @_f\n95:   .\n96:   (list\n97:     (symbol) @variable.parameter)\n98:   (#any-of? @_f \"lambda\" \"fn\"))\n99: \n100: ; --- let-binding variables ---\n101: \n102: (list\n103:   .\n104:   (symbol) @_f\n105:   .\n106:   (list\n107:     (list\n108:       (symbol) @variable.parameter))\n109:   (#any-of? @_f \"let\" \"let*\" \"letrec\" \"do\"))\n110: \n111: ; --- Define with inline parameter list: (define (name args...) body) ---\n112: \n113: (list\n114:   .\n115:   (symbol) @_f\n116:   .\n117:   (list\n118:     .\n119:     (symbol) @function\n120:     (symbol) @variable.parameter)\n121:   (#eq? @_f \"define\"))\n122: \n123: ; --- Sema builtin functions ---\n124: \n125: (list\n126:   .\n127:   (symbol) @function.builtin\n128:   (#any-of? @function.builtin\n129:     ; Higher-order / functional\n130:     \"map\" \"filter\" \"foldl\" \"foldr\" \"reduce\" \"for-each\" \"apply\"\n131:     ; LLM primitives\n132:     \"conversation/new\" \"conversation/say\"\n133:     \"conversation/messages\" \"conversation/last-reply\" \"conversation/fork\"\n134:     \"conversation/add-message\" \"conversation/model\"\n135:     \"llm/complete\" \"llm/chat\" \"llm/stream\" \"llm/send\"\n136:     \"llm/extract\" \"llm/classify\" \"llm/batch\" \"llm/pmap\"\n137:     \"llm/embed\" \"llm/auto-configure\" \"llm/configure\"\n138:     \"llm/set-budget\" \"llm/budget-remaining\"\n139:     \"llm/define-provider\" \"llm/last-usage\" \"llm/session-usage\"\n140:     \"llm/similarity\" \"llm/clear-budget\"\n141:     \"llm/configure-embeddings\" \"llm/current-provider\" \"llm/list-providers\"\n142:     \"llm/pricing-status\" \"llm/reset-usage\" \"llm/set-default\" \"llm/set-pricing\"\n143:     \"prompt/append\" \"prompt/messages\" \"prompt/set-system\"\n144:     \"message/role\" \"message/content\"\n145:     \"agent/run\" \"agent/max-turns\" \"agent/model\"\n146:     \"agent/name\" \"agent/system\" \"agent/tools\"\n147:     ; Embedding functions\n148:     \"embedding/->list\" \"embedding/length\"\n149:     \"embedding/list->embedding\" \"embedding/ref\"\n150:     ; Tool query functions\n151:     \"tool/name\" \"tool/description\" \"tool/parameters\"\n152:     ; I/O\n153:     \"display\" \"print\" \"println\" \"newline\" \"format\"\n154:     \"read\" \"read-line\" \"read-many\"\n155:     \"print-error\" \"println-error\" \"read-stdin\"\n156:     ; Lists\n157:     \"list\" \"cons\" \"car\" \"cdr\" \"first\" \"rest\" \"nth\"\n158:     \"append\" \"reverse\" \"length\" \"null?\" \"list?\" \"member\"\n159:     \"vector\" \"sort\" \"sort-by\" \"take\" \"drop\" \"zip\" \"flatten\"\n160:     \"range\" \"make-list\" \"flat-map\" \"take-while\" \"drop-while\"\n161:     \"every\" \"any\" \"partition\" \"last\" \"iota\"\n162:     ; ca*r/cd*r variants\n163:     \"caar\" \"cadr\" \"cdar\" \"cddr\"\n164:     \"caaar\" \"caadr\" \"cadar\" \"caddr\" \"cdaar\" \"cdadr\" \"cddar\" \"cdddr\"\n165:     ; list/* namespaced functions\n166:     \"list/chunk\" \"list/dedupe\" \"list/drop-while\" \"list/group-by\"\n167:     \"list/index-of\" \"list/interleave\" \"list/max\" \"list/min\"\n168:     \"list/pick\" \"list/repeat\" \"list/shuffle\" \"list/split-at\"\n169:     \"list/sum\" \"list/take-while\" \"list/unique\"\n170:     \"list->bytevector\" \"list->string\" \"list->vector\"\n171:     ; Currying and Partial Application ===\n5: \n6: (define (curry2 f)\n7:   (fn (a) (fn (b) (f a b))))\n8: \n9: (define (curry3 f)\n10:   (fn (a) (fn (b) (fn (c) (f a b c)))))\n11: \n12: (define add (curry2 +))\n13: (define add5 (add 5))\n14: (println (format \"Curried add5(3) = ~a\" (add5 3)))\n15: \n16: (define multiply (curry2 *))\n17: (define double (multiply 2))\n18: (define triple (multiply 3))\n19: (println (format \"double(7) = ~a, triple(7) = ~a\" (double 7) (triple 7)))\n20: \n21: ;; === 2. Function Composition Pipeline ===\n22: \n23: (define (compose . fns)\n24:   (foldl (fn (acc f) (fn (x) (acc (f x)))) (fn (x) x) fns))\n25: \n26: (define (pipe . fns)\n27:   (foldl (fn (acc f) (fn (x) (f (acc x)))) (fn (x) x) fns))\n28: \n29: (define process\n30:   (pipe\n31:     (fn (x) (* x 2))        ;; double\n32:     (fn (x) (+ x 10))       ;; add 10\n33:     (fn (x) (* x x))))      ;; square\n34: \n35: (println (format \"pipe(double, +10, square)(5) = ~a\" (process 5)))\n36: ;; 5 → 10 → 20 → 400\n37: \n38: ;; === 3. GENERIC FALLBACKS\n; =====================================================================\n\n(symbol) @variable\n\n; Generic function call: first symbol in a list\n(list\n  .\n  (symbol) @function)\n\n; =====================================================================\n; LITERALS\n; =====================================================================\n\n(number) @number\n(boolean) @boolean\n(character) @constant\n(string) @string\n(escape_sequence) @escape\n\n; =====================================================================\n; COMMENTS\n; =====================================================================\n\n(comment) @comment\n(block_comment) @comment\n\n; =====================================================================\n; PUNCTUATION\n; =====================================================================\n\n[\"(\" \")\" \"[\" \"]\" \"{\" \"}\"] @punctuation.bracket\n\n; =====================================================================\n; QUOTE OPERATORS\n; =====================================================================\n\n(quote \"'\") @operator\n(unquote_splicing \",@\") @operator\n(unquote \",\") @operator\n(quasiquote \"`\") @operator\n\n; =====================================================================\n; SPECIAL VARIABLES\n; =====================================================================\n\n((symbol) @variable.builtin\n  (#any-of? @variable.builtin \"...\" \".\"))\n\n; =====================================================================\n; OPERATORS\n; =====================================================================\n\n((symbol) @operator\n  (#any-of? @operator \"+\" \"-\" \"*\" \"/\" \"=\" \">\" \"<\" \">=\" \"<=\" \"eq?\" \"equal?\"))\n\n; =====================================================================\n; KEYWORD LITERALS :foo\n; =====================================================================\n\n((symbol) @string.special\n  (#match? @string.special \"^:\"))\n\n; =====================================================================\n; NIL\n; =====================================================================\n\n((symbol) @constant.builtin\n  (#eq? @constant.builtin \"nil\"))\n\n; =====================================================================\n; BOOLEAN SYMBOLS\n; =====================================================================\n\n((symbol) @boolean\n  (#any-of? @boolean \"true\" \"false\"))\n\n; =====================================================================\n; DEFINE SIMPLE VARIABLE: (define name value)\n; =====================================================================\n\n(list\n  .\n  (symbol) @_f\n  .\n  (symbol) @variable\n  (#eq? @_f \"define\"))\n\n; =====================================================================\n; DEFUN: (defun name ...)\n; =====================================================================\n\n(list\n  .\n  (symbol) @_f\n  .\n  (symbol) @function\n  (#eq? @_f \"defun\"))\n\n; =====================================================================\n; LAMBDA / FN PARAMETER LISTS\n; =====================================================================\n\n(list\n  .\n  (symbol) @_f\n  .\n  (list\n    (symbol) @variable.parameter)\n  (#any-of? @_f \"lambda\" \"fn\"))\n\n; =====================================================================\n; DEFINE WITH INLINE PARAMETER LIST: (define (name args...) body)\n; =====================================================================\n\n(list\n  .\n  (symbol) @_f\n  .\n  (list\n    .\n    (symbol) @function\n    (symbol) @variable.parameter)\n  (#eq? @_f \"define\"))\n\n; ## Assistant

Now I understand the full picture. `highlight_at()` returns the **first** matching token at a column (since tokens are sorted by start_col). So with `cursor.captures()`, later patterns that match the same node will push tokens **after** the earlier ones, but `highlight_at` returns the **first** one found. This means the current query ordering (generic fallbacks first) is actually correct for "first match wins" — but the specific patterns pushed later will be ignored!

The fix is to reverse the order: put specific patterns **before** generic fallbacks. Let me also look at what `cursor.captures()` actually does with ordering when multiple patterns match the same node:

```
## 1) TL;DR
Your editor is effectively **FIRST-match-wins per node** (because `highlight_at()` returns the first covering token, and ties are resolved by query pattern order). So the Helix-derived query is inverted: **generic fallbacks must go last**, and all “more specific” matches (keywords, operators, builtins, define targets, params, etc.) must come first. Also: avoid producing any wide-span captures (e.g. `list`) because they will eclipse inner tokens after sorting.

---

## 2) Recommended approach (simple path)

### A. Understand the winning semantics in *this* pipeline
Given your facts:

- Tree-sitter emits captures in document order; when multiple patterns match the **same node**, captures are emitted in **pattern order**.
- You then sort tokens by `(start_col, end_col)`.
- `highlight_at(col)` returns the **first** token whose range contains `col`.

Therefore the winner at a column is determined by:

1) **Smallest `start_col` that still contains `col`** wins (because it appears earlier in sorted tokens).
2) If two captures have the **same range** (same node), then the one whose **pattern appears earlier in the query** wins (because sorting ties don’t re-order them).

This means: any node that matches both `(symbol) @variable` and something more specific will *always* be `@variable` if that rule appears first.

### B. Fix the query ordering: “most specific first → fallbacks last”
Reorder the query into layers that reflect how conflicts happen:

1) **Leaf literals and comments** (rarely conflict; safe early)
2) **Symbol-specializations** that must beat generic `symbol` fallback:
   - keyword-literals `:foo` (and keyword-as-function `(:foo m)`)
   - special vars `.` and `...`
   - operators like `+`, `<=`, `equal?`
   - `nil`, `true/false` (symbol forms)
3) **Head-of-list special forms** (must beat generic function call head)
4) **Definition/Binding forms** that highlight *the defined name* or *bound vars* (`define`, `defun`, `lambda`, `let`, `set!`, etc.)
5) **Builtins in call position** (must beat generic function call head)
6) **Namespaced call heuristic** (e.g. `string/join`) if you want differentiation
7) **Generic call head**
8) **Final fallback: any remaining symbol is a variable**

Concretely, move these to the bottom:
```scm
(list . (symbol) @function)
(symbol) @variable
```

### C. Add/adjust Sema-specific patterns you called out

#### 1) Hashmap literal keys `{ :name "Ada" ... }`
Since tree-sitter-racket likely parses `{ ... }` as a normal `list` (just with `{}` tokens), you can’t reliably detect “this list was braced” in a highlights query. The pragmatic approach: treat **all `:foo` symbols as map keys / keywords** (e.g. `@property`) everywhere, and optionally treat `(:foo x)` as a “method-like” call.

Recommended changes:
- Prefer `@property` over `@string.special` for `:foo` because it reads like “map key / keyword”.
- Add a *more specific* rule for `(:foo person)` before the generic `:foo` rule, so the call-site can be distinct.

#### 2) Keyword args `:foo`
Covered by the same `:foo` symbol rule; it will also color `:age` in `(assoc person :age 37)` and keys in `{...}`.

#### 3) `#t` / `#f`
If tree-sitter-racket produces `(boolean)` nodes for these (typical), your existing `(boolean) @boolean` is enough. If Sema also allows `true/false` as symbols, keep your symbol-boolean rule (but it must come before `(symbol) @variable`).

#### 4) Dotted pairs `(a . b)` and variadic args `(lambda (x . rest) ...)`
This depends on how tree-sitter-racket models `.`:
- If `.` is just a `symbol` node `"."`, your existing special-variable rule already catches it, but the “rest” identifier won’t be highlighted as a parameter unless you add a specific variadic-params pattern.
- If `.` is a raw token / separate node type, you need an explicit punctuation rule. (Without the grammar AST it’s hard to be exact, but you can still add safe patterns that won’t harm if they don’t match.)

Add two things:
- Highlight dot as delimiter when it appears as a standalone symbol: `"."` → `@punctuation.delimiter` (or keep as builtin variable; delimiter tends to read better).
- Add a lambda/fn param-list variant that marks the “rest” name as `@variable.parameter`.

---

## 3) Suggested query skeleton (with correct ordering)

Below is a **re-ordered + improved** skeleton. Keep your huge builtin list as-is, just relocate it into the right layer.

```scm
; ============================================================
; 0) COMMENTS (safe first)
; ============================================================
(comment) @comment
(block_comment) @comment

; ============================================================
; 1) LITERALS (safe first)
; ============================================================
(string) @string
(escape_sequence) @escape
(number) @number
(boolean) @boolean
(character) @constant

; symbol-forms of booleans/nil used by Sema
((symbol) @boolean (#any-of? @boolean "true" "false"))
((symbol) @constant.builtin (#eq? @constant.builtin "nil"))

; ============================================================
; 2) QUOTE OPERATORS / PUNCTUATION (generally non-conflicting)
; ============================================================
(quote "'") @operator
(unquote_splicing ",@") @operator
(unquote ",") @operator
(quasiquote "`") @operator

["(" ")" "[" "]" "{" "}"] @punctuation.bracket

; If '.' is tokenized as a bare symbol, prefer delimiter styling
((symbol) @punctuation.delimiter (#eq? @punctuation.delimiter "."))

; ============================================================
; 3) SYMBOL SPECIALIZATIONS (must beat fallback symbol)
; ============================================================

; Special vars
((symbol) @variable.builtin
  (#any-of? @variable.builtin "..."))

; Operators (ensure this comes before generic call head + symbol fallback)
((symbol) @operator
  (#any-of? @operator
    "+" "-" "*" "/" "=" ">" "<" ">=" "<=" "eq?" "equal?"))

; Keyword-as-function accessor: (:name person)
(list
  .
  ((symbol) @function.method (#match? @function.method "^:")))

; Keyword literals / map keys / keyword args: :foo
((symbol) @property
  (#match? @property "^:"))

; ============================================================
; 4) SPECIAL FORMS (head position only)
; ============================================================

; Threading macros
(list . (symbol) @keyword.operator
  (#any-of? @keyword.operator "->" "->>" "as->"))

; Module / import / export
(list . (symbol) @keyword
  (#any-of? @keyword "import" "module" "export" "load"))

; Exceptions
(list . (symbol) @keyword
  (#any-of? @keyword "try" "catch" "throw"))

; Conditionals
(list . (symbol) @keyword
  (#any-of? @keyword "if" "cond" "case" "when" "unless" "else"))

; LLM special forms
(list . (symbol) @keyword.function
  (#any-of? @keyword.function "defagent" "deftool"))

; General special forms
(list . (symbol) @keyword
  (#any-of? @keyword
    "define" "defun" "lambda" "fn" "set!"
    "let" "let*" "letrec" "begin" "do"
    "and" "or"
    "quote" "quasiquote" "unquote" "unquote-splicing"
    "define-record-type" "defmacro"
    "delay" "force" "eval" "macroexpand"
    "with-budget"
    "prompt" "message"))

; ============================================================
; 5) BINDING / DEFINITIONS (highlight the bound names)
; ============================================================

; (define name value)
(list . (symbol) @_kw . (symbol) @variable
  (#eq? @_kw "define"))

; (set! name value)
(list . (symbol) @_kw . (symbol) @variable
  (#eq? @_kw "set!"))

; (defun name ...)
(list . (symbol) @_kw . (symbol) @function
  (#eq? @_kw "defun"))

; (defmacro name ...)
(list . (symbol) @_kw . (symbol) @function
  (#eq? @_kw "defmacro"))

; (define (fname args...) body...)
(list
  . (symbol) @_kw
  . (list
      . (symbol) @function
        (symbol) @variable.parameter)
  (#eq? @_kw "define"))

; (lambda (a b) ...)
(list
  . (symbol) @_kw
  . (list (symbol) @variable.parameter)
  (#any-of? @_kw "lambda" "fn"))

; Optional: variadic params if '.' is represented as a symbol node
; (lambda (a . rest) ...)
(list
  . (symbol) @_kw
  . (list
      (symbol) @variable.parameter
      (symbol) @_dot
      (symbol) @variable.parameter)
  (#any-of? @_kw "lambda" "fn")
  (#eq? @_dot "."))

; let bindings
(list
  . (symbol) @_kw
  . (list
      (list (symbol) @variable.parameter))
  (#any-of? @_kw "let" "let*" "letrec" "do"))

; ============================================================
; 6) BUILTINS + NAMESPACES (call head)
; ============================================================

; Sema builtin functions (your large #any-of? list)
(list . (symbol) @function.builtin
  (#any-of? @function.builtin ...))

; Namespaced calls like string/join (non-builtin): style as method-ish
(list . ((symbol) @function.method
          (#match? @function.method "^[^\\s]+/.+")))

; ============================================================
; 7) GENERIC FALLBACKS (MUST BE LAST)
; ============================================================

; Generic function call: first symbol in a list
(list . (symbol) @function)

; Any remaining symbol
(symbol) @variable
```

Notes:
- The `(:foo ...)` rule **must appear before** the generic `:foo` rule or it will never win (same node range, earlier pattern wins).
- The builtin rule must appear before the namespaced heuristic, or every builtin with `/` will become `@function.method` instead of `@function.builtin`.

---

## 4) Better use of `_`-prefixed captures
You’re already doing the key thing: `@_f` / `@_kw` helper captures for predicates. Keep that pattern everywhere you need to *inspect* a symbol but not highlight it.

Two practical guardrails:
- Never introduce non-underscore helper captures like `@f` or `@kw`; if your token collector doesn’t filter unknown capture names early, you can accidentally create tokens with “no color” that still participate in ordering.
- Avoid capturing large nodes (e.g. `(list) @something`) unless you truly want to color the whole form—because after sorting, that token will start earlier and eclipse interior tokens in `highlight_at()`.

---

## 5) Differentiation improvements (namespaced calls, keyword-accessors)
- **Namespaced call heads** (`string/join`, `map/entries`) are very common in Sema; using `@function.method` for non-builtin namespaced heads is a simple, high-signal differentiation that works well with FIRST-match-wins.
- **Keyword accessors** `(:name person)` are also high-signal; coloring them as `@function.method` (call-site) while keeping `:name` elsewhere as `@property` gives a nice “map access” feel.

---

## 6) Risks and guardrails
- **Variadic/dotted pairs**: exact matching depends on the tree-sitter-racket AST shape for `.`. If your patterns don’t match, nothing breaks, but you won’t get the added highlighting. Validate quickly by dumping the parse tree for a dotted example.
- **Brace-map detection**: if `{...}` is parsed as a generic list, you can’t perfectly isolate “map literals” in highlights queries alone. Coloring `:foo` as `@property` globally is the best low-risk approximation.

---

## 7) When to consider the advanced path
Only revisit the rendering pipeline (instead of query ordering) if you hit either trigger:
- You want **true “most specific wins”** semantics that can’t be expressed by ordering (e.g. prioritize narrower ranges, or explicit priority per capture).
- You want to support **overlapping region tokens** (e.g. highlight the entire list form background + still highlight symbols inside).

Advanced path (outline only): change `highlight_at()` to pick the “best” token among all containing tokens (e.g. smallest range, or highest priority), instead of the first. This is higher risk and requires defining a stable priority model.

---

**Effort estimate:** **M (1–3h)** to reorder, add the missing Sema patterns, and validate against `hello.sema` / `data-pipeline.sema` plus a small dotted/variadic test snippet.
```

  "content": "; Sema highlight queries for Token editor\n; Uses tree-sitter-racket (Scheme) grammar\n;\n; Token editor uses FIRST-MATCH-WINS semantics:\n;   - Multiple captures for the same node are sorted by (start_col, end_col)\n;   - highlight_at() returns the first matching token\n;   - Earlier patterns in this file win over later ones for the same node\n;\n; Therefore: MOST SPECIFIC patterns go FIRST, generic fallbacks go LAST.\n\n; =====================================================================\n; 1. COMMENTS & LITERALS (leaf nodes, rarely conflict)\n; =====================================================================\n\n(comment) @comment\n(block_comment) @comment\n\n(string) @string\n(escape_sequence) @escape\n(number) @number\n(boolean) @boolean\n(character) @constant\n\n; =====================================================================\n; 2. PUNCTUATION & QUOTE OPERATORS\n; =====================================================================\n\n[\"(\" \")\" \"[\" \"]\" \"{\" \"}\"] @punctuation.bracket\n\n(quote \"'\") @operator\n(unquote_splicing \",@\") @operator\n(unquote \",\") @operator\n(quasiquote \"`\") @operator\n\n; =====================================================================\n; 3. SYMBOL SPECIALIZATIONS (must come before generic symbol fallback)\n; =====================================================================\n\n; Boolean symbols (Scheme grammar parses true/false as symbols)\n((symbol) @boolean\n  (#any-of? @boolean \"true\" \"false\"))\n\n; nil\n((symbol) @constant.builtin\n  (#eq? @constant.builtin \"nil\"))\n\n; Dot as punctuation delimiter (variadic args, dotted pairs)\n((symbol) @punctuation.delimiter\n  (#eq? @punctuation.delimiter \".\"))\n\n; Ellipsis\n((symbol) @variable.builtin\n  (#eq? @variable.builtin \"...\"))\n\n; Arithmetic and comparison operators\n((symbol) @operator\n  (#any-of? @operator\n    \"+\" \"-\" \"*\" \"/\" \"%\" \"=\" \">\" \"<\" \">=\" \"<=\"\n    \"eq?\" \"equal?\" \"eqv?\"))\n\n; Keyword accessor in call position: (:name person)\n(list\n  .\n  ((symbol) @property\n    (#match? @property \"^:\")))\n\n; Keyword literals / map keys: :foo (non-call position)\n((symbol) @property\n  (#match? @property \"^:\"))\n\n; =====================================================================\n; 4. ## Assistant

All tests pass. Here's a summary of all the improvements made across both projects:

#### Token editor (`queries/sema/highlights.scm`) — rewritten for first-match-wins

| Improvement | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| **Pattern ordering** | Generic fallbacks first (broken — always won) | Specific patterns first, fallbacks last |
| **`:foo` keywords** | `@string.special` | `@property` (better semantic fit for map keys) |
| **`:foo` in call position** | Not distinguished | `@property` (keyword accessor like `(:name person)`) |
| **`"."` dot** | `@variable.builtin` | `@punctuation.delimiter` (dotted pairs/variadics) |
| **`"..."` ellipsis** | Grouped with `"."` | Separate `@variable.builtin` rule |
| **`set!` target** | Not highlighted | Bound name highlighted as `@variable` |
| **`defmacro` name** | Not highlighted | Name highlighted as `@function` |
| **`defagent`/`deftool` names** | Not highlighted | Names highlighted as `@function` |
| **`eqv?`, `%`** | Missing | Added to operators |

#### Sema-lisp (`editors/helix/queries/sema/highlights.scm`) — same new patterns added

Same improvements applied with Helix-compatible capture names (last-match-wins order preserved):
- Added `"."` → `@punctuation.delimiter`, `"..."` separated
- Added keyword accessor `(:foo ...)` → `@function.method`
- Added `set!`, `defmacro`, `defagent`, `deftool` name highlighting
- Added `eqv?`, `%` to operators

```
Tree-sitter is primarily used for **syntax highlighting** with **language injection** (e.g., Markdown fenced blocks, HTML scripts/styles). While there are design documents for advanced features like code folding and scope-based indent guides, the current implementation is focused on highlighting.

### Core Tree-sitter Usage
- **Syntax Highlighting**: Uses `QueryCursor` and `captures` to extract tokens based on `.scm` files and built-in grammar queries.
- **Language Injection**: Processes Markdown fenced code blocks and HTML `<script>`/`<style>` tags by parsing nested content with secondary parsers.
- **Incremental Parsing**: The `DocParseState` caches the `Tree` per document and uses `compute_incremental_edit` to apply `InputEdit` for performance.

### Relevant Files:
- [src/syntax/parser.rs#L129-L1100](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/parser.rs#L129-L1100) — Main `ParserState` managing `Tree` and `Query` objects for highlighting and injections.
- [src/syntax/parser.rs#L53-L91](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/parser.rs#L53-L91) — Implementation of `InputEdit` computation for incremental parsing.
- [src/syntax/highlights.rs#L9-L150](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/highlights.rs#L9-L150) — Mapping Tree-sitter capture names to theme-specific IDs.

### Planned Features (Doc references):
- [docs/feature/indent-guides.md#L97-L162](file://~/code/token-editor/docs/feature/indent-guides.md#L97-L162) — Design for Tree-sitter based scope detection.
- [docs/feature/folding-advanced.md#L28-L45](file://~/code/token-editor/docs/feature/folding-advanced.md#L28-L45) — Design for AST-based code folding.
```

```
Comment toggling (line and block comments) is not yet fully implemented in the core editor logic, although it is extensively referenced in architectural plans, documentation, and keybinding configurations.

### Current Status
- **Commands & Keybindings**: Commands like `editor.comment_line` and `CommentLines` (bound to `Cmd+K Cmd+C`) are defined in the keymap system and documentation, but they do not yet map to active implementation in the message update loop.
- **Syntax Highlighting**: Comment tokens are correctly identified via Tree-sitter for many languages (Rust, JavaScript, Python, etc.) and styled in themes.
- **Language Configuration**: The current `LanguageId` system (in `src/syntax/languages.rs`) identifies languages but does not yet store metadata like comment prefixes (`//`, `#`, `;`) or block comment markers.
- **Future Plans**: Documentation indicates plans for "Smart line start" toggling and "Toggle Comment" as a future UX enhancement.

### Relevant Files
- [src/keymap/command.rs#L596](file://~/code/token-editor/src/keymap/command.rs#L596) - Command definitions for toggling.
- [src/syntax/languages.rs#L9-L39](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/languages.rs#L9-L39) - Supported languages (where configuration will likely be added).
- [src/syntax/highlights.rs#L14](file://~/code/token-editor/src/syntax/highlights.rs#L14) - Comment token definition for highlighting.
- [src/messages.rs#L116-L147](file://~/code/token-editor/src/messages.rs#L116-L147) - `DocumentMsg` variants (lacks a `ToggleComment` variant).
- [docs/future/keymap-enhancements.md#L44-L45](file://~/code/token-editor/docs/future/keymap-enhancements.md#L44-L45) - Proposed keybindings for comment toggling.
- [docs/feature/folding-advanced.md#L201](file://~/code/token-editor/docs/feature/folding-advanced.md#L201) - Plans for cross-language comment marker support.
```

## Assistant

Based on the investigation, here's what's available and what's not:

**Currently in Token editor via tree-sitter:** only syntax highlighting + language injection (Markdown/HTML). Folding, indent guides, comment toggling, and text objects are all **planned but not yet implemented**.

So for Sema specifically, there's nothing more we can add via tree-sitter queries right now — the editor only consumes `highlights.scm`. The improvements we already made cover everything the engine supports.

**What would become useful once features land:**

| Future Feature | What Sema would need |
|---|---|
| **Comment toggling** | Language metadata: `; ` line prefix, `#| |#` block markers |
| **Code folding** | Fold on `(list)` nodes (every top-level form, `define`, `let`, `cond` blocks) |
| **Indent guides** | Scope detection on `(list)` depth — Sema's indentation is purely structural |
| **Bracket matching** | Already works generically via `()[]{}` — no Sema-specific work needed |
| **Text objects** | Select inner/outer `(list)` — s-expression based, very natural for Lisps |

None of these have a query or config surface in the editor yet. The highlighting query we built is comprehensive for the current capabilities. Want me to add any language metadata to `LanguageId` (like comment prefixes) to prepare for when comment toggling lands?

      "text": "<loaded_skill name=\"dispatching-parallel-agents\">\n# dispatching-parallel-agents Skill\n\n\n# Dispatching Parallel Agents\n\n## Overview\n\nWhen you have multiple unrelated failures (different test files, different subsystems, different bugs), investigating them sequentially wastes time. Each investigation is independent and can happen in parallel.\n\n**Core principle:** Dispatch one agent per independent problem domain. Let them work concurrently.\n\n## When to Use\n\n```dot\ndigraph when_to_use {\n    \"Multiple failures?\" [shape=diamond];\n    \"Are they independent?\" [shape=diamond];\n    \"Single agent investigates all\" [shape=box];\n    \"One agent per problem domain\" [shape=box];\n    \"Can they work in parallel?\" [shape=diamond];\n    \"Sequential agents\" [shape=box];\n    \"Parallel dispatch\" [shape=box];\n\n    \"Multiple failures?\" -> \"Are they independent?\" [label=\"yes\"];\n    \"Are they independent?\" -> \"Single agent investigates all\" [label=\"no - related\"];\n    \"Are they independent?\" -> \"Can they work in parallel?\" [label=\"yes\"];\n    \"Can they work in parallel?\" -> \"Parallel dispatch\" [label=\"yes\"];\n    \"Can they work in parallel?\" -> \"Sequential agents\" [label=\"no - shared state\"];\n}\n```\n\n**Use when:**\n- 3+ test files failing with different root causes\n- Multiple subsystems broken independently\n- Each problem can be understood without context from others\n- No shared state between investigations\n\n**Don't use when:**\n- Failures are related (fix one might fix others)\n- Need to understand full system state\n- Agents would interfere with each other\n\n## The Pattern\n\n### 1. Identify Independent Domains\n\nGroup failures by what's broken:\n- File A tests: Tool approval flow\n- File B tests: Batch completion behavior\n- File C tests: Abort functionality\n\nEach domain is independent - fixing tool approval doesn't affect abort tests.\n\n### 2. Create Focused Agent Tasks\n\nEach agent gets:\n- **Specific scope:** One test file or subsystem\n- **Clear goal:** Make these tests pass\n- **Constraints:** Don't change other code\n- **Expected output:** Summary of what you found and fixed\n\n### 3. Dispatch in Parallel\n\n```typescript\n// In Claude Code / AI environment\nTask(\"Fix agent-tool-abort.test.ts failures\")\nTask(\"Fix batch-completion-behavior.test.ts failures\")\nTask(\"Fix tool-approval-race-conditions.test.ts failures\")\n// All three run concurrently\n```\n\n### 4. Review and Integrate\n\nWhen agents return:\n- Read each summary\n- Verify fixes don't conflict\n- Run full test suite\n- Integrate all changes\n\n## Agent Prompt Structure\n\nGood agent prompts are:\n1. **Focused** - One clear problem domain\n2. **Self-contained** - All context needed to understand the problem\n3. **Specific about output** - What should the agent return?\n\n```markdown\nFix the 3 failing tests in src/agents/agent-tool-abort.test.ts:\n\n1. \"should abort tool with partial output capture\" - expects 'interrupted at' in message\n2. \"should handle mixed completed and aborted tools\" - fast tool aborted instead of completed\n3. \"should properly track pendingToolCount\" - expects 3 results but gets 0\n\nThese are timing/race condition issues. Your task:\n\n1. Read the test file and understand what each test verifies\n2. Identify root cause - timing issues or actual bugs?\n3. Fix by:\n   - Replacing arbitrary timeouts with event-based waiting\n   - Fixing bugs in abort implementation if found\n   - Adjusting test expectations if testing changed behavior\n\nDo NOT just increase timeouts - find the real issue.\n\nReturn: Summary of what you found and what you fixed.\n```\n\n## Common Mistakes\n\n**❌ Too broad:** \"Fix all the tests\" - agent gets lost\n**✅ Specific:** \"Fix agent-tool-abort.test.ts\" - focused scope\n\n**❌ No context:** \"Fix the race condition\" - agent doesn't know where\n**✅ Context:** Paste the error messages and test names\n\n**❌ No constraints:** Agent might refactor everything\n**✅ Constraints:** \"Do NOT change production code\" or \"Fix tests only\"\n\n**❌ Vague output:** \"Fix it\" - you don't know what changed\n**✅ Specific:** \"Return summary of root cause and changes\"\n\n## When NOT to Use\n\n**Related failures:** Fixing one might fix others - investigate together first\n**Need full context:** Understanding requires seeing entire system\n**Exploratory debugging:** You don't know what's broken yet\n**Shared state:** Agents would interfere (editing same files, using same resources)\n\n## Real Example from Session\n\n**Scenario:** 6 test failures across 3 files after major refactoring\n\n**Failures:**\n- agent-tool-abort.test.ts: 3 failures (timing issues)\n- batch-completion-behavior.test.ts: 2 failures (tools not executing)\n- tool-approval-race-conditions.test.ts: 1 failure (execution count = 0)\n\n**Decision:** Independent domains - abort logic separate from batch completion separate from race conditions\n\n**Dispatch:**\n```\nAgent 1 → Fix agent-tool-abort.test.ts\nAgent 2 → Fix batch-completion-behavior.test.ts\nAgent 3 → Fix tool-approval-race-conditions.test.ts\n```\n\n**Results:**\n- Agent 1: Replaced timeouts with event-based waiting\n- Agent 2: Fixed event structure bug (threadId in wrong place)\n- Agent 3: Added wait for async tool execution to complete\n\n**Integration:** All fixes independent, no conflicts, full suite green\n\n**Time saved:** 3 problems solved in parallel vs sequentially\n\n## Key Benefits\n\n1. **Parallelization** - =====================================================================\n7: ; GENERIC FALLBACKS (least specific — put first so they lose to later)\n8: ; =====================================================================\n9: \n10: ; --- Fallback: any remaining symbol is a variable ---\n11: \n12: (symbol) @variable\n13: \n14: ; --- Generic function call ---\n15: \n16: (list\n17:   .\n18:   (symbol) @function)\n19: \n20: ; =====================================================================\n21: ; INCREASINGLY SPECIFIC PATTERNS (override the fallbacks above)\n22: ; =====================================================================\n23: \n24: ; --- Literals ---\n25: \n26: (number) @constant.numeric\n27: (boolean) @constant.builtin.boolean\n28: (character) @constant.character\n29: (string) @string\n30: (escape_sequence) @constant.character.escape\n31: \n32: ; --- Comments ---\n33: \n34: (comment) @comment.line\n35: (block_comment) @comment.block\n36: \n37: ; --- Punctuation ---\n38: \n39: [\"(\" \")\" \"[\" \"]\" \"{\" \"}\"] @punctuation.bracket\n40: \n41: ; --- Quote operators ---\n42: \n43: (quote \"'\") @operator\n44: (unquote_splicing \",@\") @operator\n45: (unquote \",\") @operator\n46: (quasiquote \"`\") @operator\n47: \n48: ; --- Dot as punctuation (variadic args, dotted pairs) ---\n49: \n50: ((symbol) @punctuation.delimiter\n51:   (#eq? @punctuation.delimiter \".\"))\n52: \n53: ; --- Ellipsis ---\n54: \n55: ((symbol) @variable.builtin\n56:   (#eq? @variable.builtin \"...\"))\n57: \n58: ; --- Operators ---\n59: \n60: ((symbol) @operator\n61:   (#any-of? @operator\n62:     \"+\" \"-\" \"*\" \"/\" \"%\" \"=\" \">\" \"<\" \">=\" \"<=\"\n63:     \"eq?\" \"equal?\" \"eqv?\"))\n64: \n65: ; Provides syntax highlighting, indentation,\n20: ;; and REPL integration.\n21: ;;\n22: ;; Install:\n23: ;;   (add-to-list 'load-path \"/path/to/sema/editors/emacs\")\n24: ;;   (require 'sema-mode)\n25: ;;\n26: ;; Or with use-package:\n27: ;;   (use-package sema-mode\n28: ;;     :load-path \"/path/to/sema/editors/emacs\"\n29: ;;     :mode \"\\\\.sema\\\\'\")\n30: ;;\n31: ;; Homepage: https://sema-lang.com\n32: \n33: ;;; Code:\n34: \n35: (require 'lisp-mode)\n36: (require 'comint)\n37: \n38: ;; ── Customization ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n39: \n40: (defgroup sema nil\n41:   \"Major mode for Sema, a Lisp with LLM primitives.\"\n42:   :group 'languages\n43:   :prefix \"sema-\")\n44: \n45: (defcustom sema-program \"sema\"\n46:   \"Path to the Sema interpreter executable.\"\n47:   :type 'string\n48:   :group 'sema)\n49: \n50: ;; ── Syntax table ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n51: \n52: (defvar sema-mode-syntax-table\n53:   (let ((table (make-syntax-table)))\n54:     ;; Semicolon starts a comment, newline ends it\n55:     (modify-syntax-entry ?\\; \"<\" table)\n56:     (modify-syntax-entry ?\\n \">\" table)\n57:     ;; Double quotes for strings\n58:     (modify-syntax-entry ?\\\" \"\\\"\" table)\n59:     ;; Backslash is escape\n60:     (modify-syntax-entry ?\\\\ \"\\\\\" table)\n61:     ;; Parentheses\n62:     (modify-syntax-entry ?\\( \"()\" table)\n63:     (modify-syntax-entry ?\\) \")(\" table)\n64:     (modify-syntax-entry ?\\[ \"(]\" table)\n65:     (modify-syntax-entry ?\\] \")[\" table)\n66:     (modify-syntax-entry ?\\{ \"(}\" table)\n67:     (modify-syntax-entry ?\\} \"){\" table)\n68:     ;; Characters that are part of symbols\n69:     (modify-syntax-entry ?_ \"_\" table)\n70:     (modify-syntax-entry ?- \"_\" table)\n71:     (modify-syntax-entry ?/ \"_\" table)\n72:     (modify-syntax-entry ?? \"_\" table)\n73:     (modify-syntax-entry ?! \"_\" table)\n74:     (modify-syntax-entry ?* \"_\" table)\n75:     (modify-syntax-entry ?+ \"_\" table)\n76:     (modify-syntax-entry ?< \"_\" table)\n77:     (modify-syntax-entry ?> \"_\" table)\n78:     (modify-syntax-entry ?= \"_\" table)\n79:     (modify-syntax-entry ?: \"_\" table)\n80:     ;; Block comments: #| ... |#\n81:     (modify-syntax-entry ?# \"_ 14b\" table)\n82:     (modify-syntax-entry ?| \"_ 23b\" table)\n83:     ;; Quote-like prefixes for proper sexp handling\n84:     (modify-syntax-entry ?' \"'\" table)\n85:     (modify-syntax-entry ?` \"'\" table)\n86:     (modify-syntax-entry ?, \"'\" table)\n87:     table)\n88:   \"Syntax table for `sema-mode'.\")\n89: \n90: ;; ── Font-lock (syntax highlighting) ────────────────────────────────────\n91: \n92: (defvar sema-special-forms\n93:   '(\"define\" \"defun\" \"lambda\" \"fn\" \"if\" \"cond\" \"case\" \"when\" \"unless\"\n94:     \"let\" \"let*\" \"letrec\" \"begin\" \"do\" \"and\" \"or\"\n95:     \"set!\" \"quote\" \"quasiquote\" \"unquote\" \"unquote-splicing\"\n96:     \"define-record-type\" \"defmacro\" \"defagent\" \"deftool\"\n97:     \"try\" \"catch\" \"throw\"\n98:     \"import\" \"module\" \"export\" \"load\"\n99:     \"delay\" \"force\" \"eval\" \"macroexpand\" \"with-budget\"\n100:     \"prompt\" \"message\"\n101:     \"else\")\n102:   \"Sema special forms and core keywords.\")\n103: \n104: (defvar sema-builtin-functions\n105:   '(;; Threading macros\n106:     \"->\" \"->>\" \"as->\"\n107:     ;; Higher-order\n108:     \"map\" \"filter\" \"foldl\" \"foldr\" \"reduce\" \"for-each\" \"apply\"\n109:     ;; LLM / Agent\n110:     \"conversation/new\" \"conversation/say\"\n111:     \"conversation/messages\" \"conversation/last-reply\" \"conversation/fork\"\n112:     \"conversation/add-message\" \"conversation/model\"\n113:     \"llm/complete\" \"llm/chat\" \"llm/stream\" \"llm/send\"\n114:     \"llm/extract\" \"llm/classify\" \"llm/batch\" \"llm/pmap\"\n115:     \"llm/embed\" \"llm/auto-configure\" \"llm/configure\"\n116:     \"llm/set-budget\" \"llm/budget-remaining\"\n117:     \"llm/define-provider\" \"llm/last-usage\" \"llm/session-usage\"\n118:     \"llm/similarity\" \"llm/clear-budget\"\n119:     \"llm/configure-embeddings\" \"llm/current-provider\" \"llm/list-providers\"\n120:     \"llm/pricing-status\" \"llm/reset-usage\" \"llm/set-default\" \"llm/set-pricing\"\n121:     \"prompt/append\" \"prompt/messages\" \"prompt/set-system\"\n122:     \"message/role\" \"message/content\"\n123:     \"agent/run\" \"agent/max-turns\" \"agent/model\"\n124:     \"agent/name\" \"agent/system\" \"agent/tools\"\n125:     ;; Embedding functions\n126:     \"embedding/->list\" \"embedding/length\"\n127:     \"embedding/list->embedding\" \"embedding/ref\"\n128:     ;; Tool query functions\n129:     \"tool/name\" \"tool/description\" \"tool/parameters\"\n130:     ;; I/O\n131:     \"display\" \"print\" \"println\" \"newline\" \"format\"\n132:     \"read\" \"read-line\" \"read-many\"\n133:     \"print-error\" \"println-error\" \"read-stdin\"\n134:     \"not\" \"error\"\n135:     ;; Lists\n136:     \"list\" \"cons\" \"car\" \"cdr\" \"first\" \"rest\" \"nth\"\n137:     \"append\" \"reverse\" \"length\" \"null?\" \"list?\" \"member\"\n138:     \"vector\" \"sort\" \"sort-by\" \"take\" \"drop\" \"zip\" \"flatten\"\n139:     \"range\" \"make-list\" \"flat-map\" \"take-while\" \"drop-while\"\n140:     \"every\" \"any\" \"partition\" \"iota\" \"last\"\n141:     ;; ca*r/cd*r variants\n142:     \"caar\" \"cadr\" \"cdar\" \"cddr\"\n143:     \"caaar\" \"caadr\" \"cadar\" \"caddr\" \"cdaar\" \"cdadr\" \"cddar\" \"cdddr\"\n144:     ;; list/* namespaced functions\n145:     \"list/chunk\" \"list/dedupe\" \"list/drop-while\" \"list/group-by\"\n146:     \"list/index-of\" \"list/interleave\" \"list/max\" \"list/min\"\n147:     \"list/pick\" \"list/repeat\" \"list/shuffle\" \"list/split-at\"\n148:     \"list/sum\" \"list/take-while\" \"list/unique\"\n149:     \"list->bytevector\" \"list->string\" \"list->vector\"\n150:     ;; Additional list functions\n151:     \"assq\" \"assv\" \"flatten-deep\" \"frequencies\" \"interpose\" \"vector->list\"\n152:     ;; Strings\n153:     \"string-append\" \"string/join\" \"string/split\"\n154:     \"string/trim\" \"string/upper\" \"string/lower\"\n155:     \"string/contains?\" \"string/starts-with?\" \"string/ends-with?\"\n156:     \"string/replace\" \"substring\" \"string-length\" \"string-ref\"\n157:     \"string/capitalize\" \"string/empty?\" \"string/index-of\"\n158:     \"string/reverse\" \"string/repeat\"\n159:     \"string/pad-left\" \"string/pad-right\"\n160:     \"str\" \"string->keyword\" \"keyword->string\"\n161:     \"string->char\" \"string->float\" \"string->list\" \"string->utf8\"\n162:     \"string-ci=?\"\n163:     \"string/byte-length\" \"string/chars\" \"string/codepoints\"\n164:     \"string/foldcase\" \"string/from-codepoints\" \"string/last-index-of\"\n165:     \"string/map\" \"string/normalize\" \"string/number?\"\n166:     \"string/title-case\" \"string/trim-left\" \"string/trim-right\"\n167:     ;; Char functions\n168:     \"char->integer\" \"char->string\" \"integer->char\"\n169:     \"char-alphabetic?\" \"char-ci<?\" \"char-ci<=?\" \"char-ci=?\"\n170:     \"char-ci>?\" \"char-ci>=?\" \"char-downcase\" \"char-lower-case?\"\n171:     \"char-numeric?\" \"char-upcase\" \"char-upper-case?\"\n172:     \"char-whitespace?\" \"char<?\" \"char<=?\" \"char=?\" \"char>?\" \"char>=?\"\n173:     ;;
